  Numbers 15:6-9 — Signalling-day calf and ram flour: The three tenths for the calf and two tenths for the ram repeat the earlier animal-specific flour measures.

Numbers 29:4
and one tenth for each of the seven lambs.
  Numbers 15:4-5 — Signalling-day lamb flour: The tenth of flour for each of seven lambs follows the earlier per-lamb prescription without collapsing the seven animals into one.

Numbers 29:5
And also offer one male goat for sin, to make atonement for you,
  Numbers 28:15 — Sin goat alongside whole burnt offerings: Both observances prescribe one male goat for sin in addition to their whole burnt and continual offerings, while their calendar occasions remain distinct.

Numbers 29:6
besides the whole burnt offerings of the new moon, their sacrifices and their drink offerings, and the continual whole burnt offerings, their sacrifices and their drink offerings, according to their prescription, as a sweet savour unto the Lord.
  Numbers 28:11-15 — New-moon offerings expressly additional: The first-of-seventh-month offerings are explicitly besides the new-moon whole burnt offerings and accompaniments specified in the preceding chapter.
  Numbers 28:3-8 — Daily offerings also remain additional: The signalling-day offerings do not replace the continual lambs and their sacrifices and drink offerings, which remain expressly required alongside them.

Numbers 29:7
And on the tenth day of this month you shall have a holy appointed assembly; and you shall afflict your souls, and shall do no work.
  Leviticus 23:27-32 — Exact atonement-day calendar and restraint: Both laws specify the tenth day of the seventh month, a holy assembly, affliction of souls, and abstention from work.

Numbers 29:8
And you shall present whole burnt offerings as a sweet savour unto the Lord, offerings: one calf from the cattle, one ram, and seven year-old lambs; they shall be unblemished for you.
  Numbers 29:2 — Same-chapter identical animal groups on distinct days: The tenth-day whole burnt group matches the first-day calf, ram, and seven lambs, while their calendrical and ritual settings remain different.

Numbers 29:9
Their sacrifice shall be fine flour mixed with oil: three tenths for the calf and two tenths for the one ram,
  Numbers 15:6-9 — Tenth-day calf and ram flour: The three-tenths calf and two-tenths ram flour accompaniments retain the earlier animal-specific prescription.

Numbers 29:10
and one tenth for each of the seven lambs.
  Numbers 15:4-5 — Tenth-day individual lamb measure: Each of the seven lambs receives a tenth of flour, corresponding to the earlier per-lamb measure.

Numbers 29:11
And also offer one male goat for sin, to make atonement for you, besides the sin offering of atonement, the continual whole burning, its sacrifice and its drink offering, according to the prescription, as a sweet savour, an offering unto the Lord.
  Leviticus 16:15-16 — Additional goat distinguished from atonement rite: The additional goat here is expressly besides the atonement sin offering; Leviticus details the people's sin goat whose blood enters within the veil.

Numbers 29:12
And on the fifteenth day of the seventh month you shall hold a holy appointed assembly. You shall do no laborious work and shall keep a festival unto the Lord for seven days.
  Leviticus 23:34-36 — Exact seven-day festival calendar: Both laws begin the seven-day festival on the fifteenth of the seventh month and prohibit laborious work at its opening assembly.

Numbers 29:13
And on the first day you shall present whole burnt offerings, offerings, as a sweet savour unto the Lord: thirteen calves from the cattle, two rams, and fourteen year-old lambs; they shall be unblemished.
  Numbers 29:17 — Same-chapter first and second days distinguished: The first day's thirteen calves decrease to twelve on the second, while the two rams and fourteen lambs remain the same.

Numbers 29:14
Their sacrifices shall be fine flour mixed with oil: three tenths for each of the thirteen calves and two tenths for each of the two rams,
  Numbers 15:6-9 — First-day flour scales by animal number: The three tenths for each of thirteen calves and two tenths for each of two rams preserve the earlier per-animal measures.

Numbers 29:15
and one tenth for each of the fourteen lambs.
  Numbers 15:4-5 — First-day fourteen lambs retain individual measure: Each of the fourteen first-day lambs receives a tenth of flour, applying the earlier lamb prescription separately to every animal.

Numbers 29:16
And also offer one male goat for sin, besides the continual whole burning, their sacrifices, and their drink offerings.
  Numbers 28:15 — First festival day's additional sin goat: The first festival day adds one male goat for sin besides the continual offering, paralleling the distinct sin-goat provision at the new moon.

Numbers 29:17
And on the second day offer twelve calves from the cattle, two rams, and fourteen unblemished year-old lambs.
  Numbers 29:13 — Same-chapter second day compared with first: The second day's twelve calves are one fewer than the first day's thirteen, while two rams and fourteen lambs continue unchanged.

Numbers 29:18
Their sacrifice and their drink offering for the calves, the rams, and the lambs shall be according to their number, as prescribed.
  Numbers 28:12-14 — Second-day accompaniments by animal number: The second-day sacrifices and drink offerings follow the animal counts and the specified calf, ram, and lamb measures, rather than an undifferentiated group amount.

Numbers 29:19
And also offer one male goat for sin, besides the continual whole burning, their sacrifices, and their drink offerings.
  Numbers 29:16 — Same-chapter second-day sin goat and continuing service: The second day's one male goat for sin and additional continual offerings repeat the first-day pattern, alongside its separately stated animal group.

Numbers 29:20
On the third day offer eleven calves, two rams, and fourteen unblemished year-old lambs.
  Numbers 29:17 — Same-chapter third day compared with second: The calf count decreases from twelve to eleven on the third day, while two rams and fourteen lambs remain explicitly unchanged.

Numbers 29:21
Their sacrifice and their drink offering for the calves, the rams, and the lambs shall be according to their number, as prescribed.
  Numbers 28:12-14 — Third-day accompaniments by animal number: The third-day sacrifices and drink offerings follow the stated animal numbers and the established calf, ram, and lamb accompaniment measures.

Numbers 29:22
And also offer one male goat for sin, besides the continual whole burning, their sacrifices, and their drink offerings.
  Numbers 29:16 — Same-chapter third-day additional sin goat: The third-day sin goat and continuing whole burning preserve the first-day distinction between the festival's additional offering and daily service.

Numbers 29:23
On the fourth day offer ten calves, two rams, and fourteen unblemished year-old lambs.
  Numbers 29:20 — Same-chapter fourth day compared with third: The fourth day reduces calves from eleven to ten while continuing the same two rams and fourteen unblemished year-old lambs.

Numbers 29:24
Their sacrifices and their drink offerings for the calves, the rams, and the lambs shall be according to their number, as prescribed.
  Numbers 28:12-14 — Fourth-day accompaniments by animal number: The fourth-day sacrifices and drink offerings are prescribed according to each animal category's number, using the established per-animal measures.

Numbers 29:25
And also offer one male goat for sin, besides the continual whole burning, their sacrifices, and their drink offerings.
  Numbers 29:16 — Same-chapter fourth-day additional sin goat: The fourth day likewise adds one male goat for sin beside the continual whole burning, sacrifices, and drink offerings.

Numbers 29:26
On the fifth day offer nine calves, two rams, and fourteen unblemished year-old lambs.
  Numbers 29:23 — Same-chapter fifth day compared with fourth: The fifth day's nine calves follow the fourth day's ten, while the explicitly numbered rams and lambs remain two and fourteen.

Numbers 29:27
Their sacrifices and their drink offerings for the calves, the rams, and the lambs shall be according to their number, as prescribed.
  Numbers 28:12-14 — Fifth-day accompaniments by animal number: The fifth-day sacrifices and drink offerings remain tied to the stated calf, ram, and lamb numbers and their prescribed individual measures.

Numbers 29:28
And also offer one male goat for sin, besides the continual whole burning, their sacrifices, and their drink offerings.
  Numbers 29:16 — Same-chapter fifth-day additional sin goat: The fifth-day one-goat sin offering retains the first-day additional-offering pattern without replacing the continual whole burning and its accompaniments.

Numbers 29:29
On the sixth day offer eight calves, two rams, and fourteen unblemished year-old lambs.
  Numbers 29:26 — Same-chapter sixth day compared with fifth: Calves decrease from nine to eight on the sixth day; the two rams and fourteen year-old lambs remain the same.

Numbers 29:30
Their sacrifices and their drink offerings for the calves, the rams, and the lambs shall be according to their number, as prescribed.
  Numbers 28:12-14 — Sixth-day accompaniments by animal number: The sixth day's sacrifices and drink offerings follow its animal count and the separately prescribed calf, ram, and lamb measures.

Numbers 29:31
And also offer one male goat for sin, besides the continual whole burning, their sacrifices, and their drink offerings.
  Numbers 29:16 — Same-chapter sixth-day additional sin goat: The sixth day repeats the single sin goat alongside the continual whole burning, sacrifices, and drink offerings already distinguished on the first day.

Numbers 29:32
On the seventh day offer seven calves, two rams, and fourteen unblemished year-old lambs.
  Numbers 29:29 — Same-chapter seventh day compared with sixth: The seventh day has seven calves rather than eight, while retaining two rams and fourteen unblemished year-old lambs.

Numbers 29:33
Their sacrifices and their drink offerings for the calves, the rams, and the lambs shall be according to their number, according to their prescription.
  Numbers 28:12-14 — Seventh-day accompaniments by animal number: The seventh-day sacrifices and drink offerings follow the prescribed individual measures for its stated calf, ram, and lamb numbers.

Numbers 29:34
And also offer one male goat for sin, besides the continual whole burning, their sacrifices, and their drink offerings.
  Numbers 29:16 — Same-chapter seventh-day additional sin goat: The seventh day continues the single sin goat and continual-offering additions established in the first day's prescription.

Numbers 29:35
And on the eighth day you shall hold a closing festival. You shall do no laborious work on it.
  Leviticus 23:36 — Eighth-day closing observance: Both laws specify an eighth-day closing observance and prohibit laborious work; Leviticus additionally states its holy assembly and offerings.

Numbers 29:36
And you shall present whole burnt offerings as a sweet savour, offerings unto the Lord: one calf, one ram, and seven unblemished year-old lambs.
  Numbers 29:13 — Same-chapter closing-day animal group distinguished: The closing day has one calf, one ram, and seven lambs, distinctly fewer than the opening thirteen calves, two rams, and fourteen lambs.

Numbers 29:37
Their sacrifices and their drink offerings for the calf, the ram, and the lambs shall be according to their number, as prescribed.
  Numbers 28:12-14 — Closing-day accompaniments by animal number: The closing day's sacrifices and drink offerings follow its own one-calf, one-ram, seven-lamb group and the established per-animal measures.

Numbers 29:38
And also offer one male goat for sin, besides the continual whole burning, their sacrifices, and their drink offerings.
  Numbers 29:16 — Same-chapter closing-day additional sin goat: The closing day retains one male goat for sin beside continual service, while its whole burnt group differs from the first day's.

Numbers 29:39
You shall offer these unto the Lord at your festivals, apart from your vows, your freewill offerings, your whole burnt offerings, your sacrifices, your drink offerings, and your sacrifices of deliverance.
  Leviticus 23:37-38 — Festival obligations distinguished from other gifts: Both summaries distinguish the scheduled festival offerings from additional vows and freewill gifts, preserving the separate obligations rather than substituting one for another.

Numbers 30:1
And Moses told the sons of Israel everything that the Lord had commanded Moses.
  Numbers 29:35-39 — Instruction and communication summary: Moses' report of everything commanded closes the preceding festival instructions, which distinguish their prescribed offerings from vows and freewill offerings.

Numbers 30:2
And Moses spoke to the rulers of the tribes of Israel, saying: This is the word that the Lord has commanded:
  Numbers 30:3-6 — Same-chapter address and specified law: Moses' address to tribal rulers introduces the binding force of vowed words and the particular hearing-day rules concerning a young woman in her father's house.

Numbers 30:3
Any person who vows a vow unto the Lord, or swears an oath, or imposes a restriction upon himself, shall not profane his word. He shall do everything that comes from his mouth.
  Deuteronomy 23:22-24 — Vowed words must be fulfilled: Both laws require performance of what a person has promised by the lips to the Lord, with Deuteronomy additionally warning against delay.
  Ecclesiastes 5:3-4 — Vow fulfilment: The duty to do everything spoken in a vow corresponds to Ecclesiastes' instruction to pay what one vows and its warning against vowing without payment.
  Matthew 5:33-37 — Oath obligation recalled and addressed: Jesus recalls the instruction to fulfil oaths to the Lord before directing His hearers not to swear, giving a later response to the obligation stated here.

Numbers 30:4
If a woman vows a vow unto the Lord or imposes a restriction while in her father’s house in her youth,
  Numbers 30:5-6 — Same-chapter case and alternative outcomes: The case of a young woman vowing in her father's house is followed by the distinct consequences of his silence and his refusal on the hearing day.

Numbers 30:5
and her father hears her vows and her restrictions that she imposed upon herself, but her father remains silent toward her, then all her vows shall stand, and all her restrictions that she imposed upon herself shall remain.
  Numbers 30:8 — Same-chapter silence in distinct households: Both rules establish the woman's vows when the specified household authority hears and remains silent, with father and husband distinguished.
  Numbers 30:6 — Same-chapter silence and same-day refusal contrasted: The father's silence leaves the vows standing, whereas his refusal on the day he hears prevents their standing and brings the stated cleansing.

Numbers 30:6
But if her father refuses her on the day he hears, none of her vows or restrictions that she imposed upon herself shall stand. The Lord will cleanse her, because her father refused her.
  Numbers 30:9 — Same-chapter hearing-day refusal compared: When a father or husband refuses on the day he hears, the specified vows do not stand; both rules also state the Lord's cleansing.

Numbers 30:7
If she becomes a man’s wife while her vows are upon her, according to the specific statement of her lips by which she imposed a restriction upon herself,
  Numbers 30:11 — Same-chapter timing of vows distinguished: This case begins with vows already upon a woman when she becomes a wife; the later case concerns a vow made in her husband's house.
  Numbers 30:8-9 — Same-chapter existing vows and hearing response: The vows present when the woman becomes a wife are governed by the following alternatives of the husband's hearing-day silence or refusal.

Numbers 30:8
and her husband hears, and remains silent toward her on the day he hears, then all her vows shall stand, and her restrictions that she imposed upon herself shall remain.
  Numbers 30:12 — Same-chapter husband's silence establishes vows: Both marital cases state that the husband's hearing without refusal leaves the woman's vows and self-imposed restrictions standing.

Numbers 30:9
But if her husband refuses her on the day he hears, none of her vows or restrictions that she imposed upon herself shall remain, because her husband refused her. The Lord will cleanse her.
  Numbers 30:13 — Same-chapter same-day annulment compared: Both provisions allow the husband to annul the stated vows on the day he hears and expressly say the Lord will cleanse her.

Numbers 30:10
And the vow of a widow or a divorced woman—whatever she vowed concerning herself—shall remain against her.
  Numbers 30:7-9 — Same-chapter marital conditions distinguished: The widow's or divorced woman's vow expressly remains against her, unlike the preceding new-marriage case with its stated hearing-day refusal provision.

Numbers 30:11
If her vow was made in her husband’s house, or her restriction was imposed upon herself with an oath,
  Numbers 30:12-13 — Same-chapter household case and outcomes: The vow or sworn restriction made in the husband's house is followed by the alternative effects of his silence and his same-day annulment.

Numbers 30:12
and her husband hears, and remains silent toward her, and does not refuse her, then all her vows shall stand, and all her restrictions which she imposed upon her soul shall stand against her.
  Numbers 30:15 — Same-chapter effect of silence elaborated: The rule that heard vows stand without refusal is elaborated by the explanation that silence on the hearing day establishes them.

Numbers 30:13
But if her husband utterly annuls them on the day he hears, then whatever came from her lips concerning her vows and the restrictions upon herself shall not remain against her. Her husband has annulled them, and the Lord will cleanse her.
  Numbers 30:16 — Same-chapter timely and belated annulment: Same-day annulment releases the stated vows and brings cleansing, whereas annulment after the hearing day is followed by the husband bearing his sin.

Numbers 30:14
Every vow and every oath imposing a restriction to afflict her soul—her husband may establish it, or her husband may annul it.
  Numbers 30:12-13 — Same-chapter general authority and specified cases: The husband's stated authority to establish or annul vows summarizes the preceding hearing-and-silence and same-day-annulment cases.

Numbers 30:15
But if he remains utterly silent toward her from day to day, he shall establish all her vows, and he shall establish the restrictions upon her, because he kept silence toward her on the day when he heard.
  Numbers 30:8 — Same-chapter hearing-day silence repeated: The explanation that silence on the hearing day establishes vows repeats the earlier condition that the husband hears and remains silent that day.

Numbers 30:16
But if he annuls them after the day on which he heard, he shall bear his sin.
  Numbers 30:13-15 — Same-chapter deadline and liability: The husband's bearing his sin after belated annulment contrasts with same-day annulment and the establishment of vows through hearing-day silence.

Numbers 30:17
These are the ordinances that the Lord commanded Moses between a man and his wife, and between a father and his daughter in her youth in her father’s house.
  Numbers 30:4-6 — Same-chapter summary and father's-house case: The summary's father-and-young-daughter category identifies the earlier case in which the father's hearing-day silence or refusal governs her vows.
  Numbers 30:11-16 — Same-chapter summary and marital case: The summary's husband-and-wife category identifies the preceding rules about household vows, hearing, silence, annulment, and the husband's liability after delay.

Numbers 31:1
And the Lord spoke unto Moses, saying:
  Numbers 31:2-4 — Same-chapter speech and specified commission: This speech introduces vengeance against Midian before Moses is gathered to his people and the ensuing order to equip a thousand men from each tribe.

Numbers 31:2
Exact vengeance for the sons of Israel from the Midianites. Finally you shall be gathered to your people.
  Numbers 25:17-18 — Earlier command concerning Midian: The order to exact vengeance continues the earlier command to strike the Midianites because of the deceit associated with Peor and Cozbi.
  Numbers 27:12-13 — Moses' approaching death announced: Both divine instructions announce Moses being gathered to his people after a specified remaining task, with viewing Canaan and vengeance against Midian kept distinct.

Numbers 31:3
And Moses spoke to the people, saying: Equip men from among you to draw up for battle before the Lord against Midian, to repay Midian with vengeance from the Lord.
  Numbers 25:17-18 — Hostility carried into military preparation: Moses' instruction to equip men against Midian gives military expression to the earlier command to strike Midian over the Peor affair.
  Numbers 31:5-6 — Same-chapter preparation command carried out: The equipping order is followed by the counting of twelve thousand armed men and Moses' sending them with Phinehas and the sacred equipment.

Numbers 31:4
Send a thousand from each tribe, from all the tribes of Israel, to draw up for battle.
  Numbers 31:5 — Same-chapter per-tribe order and total: The command to send one thousand from each tribe is carried out in the reported total of twelve thousand equipped men.

Numbers 31:5
And they counted out a thousand from each tribe from the thousands of Israel: twelve thousand equipped for battle.
  Numbers 31:4 — Same-chapter counted force and required allocation: The reported twelve thousand equipped men are explicitly counted as one thousand per tribe, implementing the preceding allocation rule.

Numbers 31:6
And Moses sent them, a thousand from each tribe, with their force and Phinehas son of Eleazar, son of Aaron the priest, with the sacred vessels and the signalling trumpets in their hands.
  Numbers 10:8-9 — Priestly trumpets in war: The expedition includes Phinehas and signalling trumpets, corresponding to the law assigning trumpet signals in war to Aaron's priestly sons.
  Numbers 25:7-13 — Phinehas identified in the Peor aftermath: The accompanying Phinehas son of Eleazar son of Aaron is the same named priestly figure whose action previously stopped the Peor plague and received the priesthood covenant.

Numbers 31:7
And they drew up for battle against Midian, as the Lord had commanded Moses, and killed every male.
  Numbers 31:2-3 — Same-chapter military commission carried out: The battle against Midian is described as obedience to the command of vengeance that Moses transmitted to the equipped force.
  Deuteronomy 20:13-15 — War treatment compared within stated scopes: Both passages describe killing enemy males and retaining spoil, while Deuteronomy's stated distant-city rule expressly spares women and dependents, unlike the subsequent Midian instructions.

Numbers 31:8
And they killed the kings of Midian together with their slain: Evi, Zur, Rekem, Hur, and Robok, the five kings of Midian. They also killed Balaam son of Beor with the sword together with their slain.
  Joshua 13:21-22 — Midian leaders and Balaam recalled: Joshua again names four matching Midian leaders and Balaam son of Beor among those killed, while its fifth leader is Reba rather than Numbers' Robok.

Numbers 31:9
And they took the women of Midian and their dependents as spoil, and plundered their livestock, all their possessions, and their resources.
  Numbers 31:15-18 — Same-chapter captured women and revised treatment: Taking Midian's women and dependents alive is the action Moses subsequently questions before distinguishing the groups to be killed and kept alive.

Numbers 31:10
And they burned all their towns in their settlements and their farmsteads with fire.
  Numbers 31:11-12 — Same-chapter destroyed settlements and retained spoils: While the towns and farmsteads are burned, the following notice separately describes people and livestock retained as spoils and brought to Israel's camp.

Numbers 31:11
And they took all the plunder and all their spoils, from people to livestock,
  Numbers 31:26 — Same-chapter captured categories and census: The spoils taken from people to livestock are the same two broad categories Moses, Eleazar, and the ancestral rulers are later ordered to total.

Numbers 31:12
and brought the captives, the spoils, and the plunder unto Moses, Eleazar the priest, and all the sons of Israel, at the camp at Araboth Moab, which is on the Jordan opposite Jericho.
  Numbers 22:1 — Camp beside Jordan opposite Jericho: Both camp notices specify the Jordan opposite Jericho; Numbers 31 calls the place Araboth Moab, while the earlier notice uses its own western-side-of-Moab wording.
  Numbers 31:26 — Same-chapter recipients and accounting officers: The captives and plunder are brought to Moses and Eleazar, who are then assigned the formal spoil count together with the ancestral-house rulers.

Numbers 31:13
And Moses, Eleazar the priest, and all the rulers of the congregation went out to meet them outside the camp.
  Numbers 31:19 — Same-chapter outside-camp meeting and purification: The leaders meet the returning force outside the camp, where the following instruction requires killers and those touching slain persons, together with captives, to remain during purification.

Numbers 31:14
And Moses was angry with the overseers of the force, the commanders of thousands and the commanders of hundreds who were returning from the battle formation,
  Numbers 31:15-16 — Same-chapter anger and its stated reason: Moses' anger at the returning commanders is explained by his question about the women kept alive and their stated role in the Peor offence.

Numbers 31:15
and Moses said to them: Why have you taken every female alive?
  Numbers 31:9 — Same-chapter question refers to captured women: Moses' question about keeping every female alive refers to the earlier report that the warriors took Midian's women and dependents as spoil.

Numbers 31:16
For these women, according to Balaam’s word, caused the sons of Israel to turn away and disregard the Lord’s word because of Peor, and the plague came upon the Lord’s congregation.
  Numbers 25:1-9 — Peor offence and plague recalled: Moses connects the women and Balaam's word with the Peor plague, recalling the earlier account of idolatry, sexual wrongdoing, and the plague's cessation.
  Numbers 25:17-18 — Reason for hostilities repeated: Both explanations connect hostility toward Midian with the deceit surrounding Peor and the plague, though this passage additionally names Balaam's word.

Numbers 31:17
And now kill every male among all the dependents, and kill every woman who has known a male’s bed.
  Judges 21:10-12 — Treatment by sex and stated sexual experience: Both narratives order the death of males and women who have known a male's bed, while distinguishing female dependents or young women who have not.
  Deuteronomy 20:13-15 — Different treatment of women and dependents: The Midian order includes male dependents and women with the stated sexual experience, whereas Deuteronomy's distant-city rule expressly spares women and dependent households.

Numbers 31:18
Take alive all the female dependents who have not known a male’s bed.
  Judges 21:12 — Women kept alive under matching criterion: The female dependents kept alive here have not known a male's bed, the same stated criterion identifying the young women brought alive from Jabesh-gilead.
  Numbers 31:35 — Same-chapter kept-alive category later counted: The women later counted as thirty-two thousand are expressly identified by the same not-knowing-a-man's-bed criterion that governs those taken alive here.

Numbers 31:19
And you shall camp outside the camp for seven days. Everyone who killed someone or touches someone slain shall be purified on the third day and the seventh day, both you and your captives.
  Numbers 19:16-19 — Corpse purification schedule applied: The third- and seventh-day cleansing after killing or touching slain persons applies the earlier corpse-contact and sprinkling schedule to warriors and captives.
  Numbers 19:11-12 — Seven-day corpse uncleanness: The seven-day stay outside camp and purification on the third and seventh days correspond to the general law for human corpse contact.

Numbers 31:20
And you shall thoroughly purify every covering, every leather article, every object made from goats’ hair, and every wooden article.
  Leviticus 11:32 — Articles capable of defilement: Both lists name wooden articles, garments or coverings, and skins among objects requiring cleansing, while the earlier law concerns dead creatures and this context concerns war spoils.
  Numbers 19:18 — Cleansing affected articles: The command to purify spoils' coverings and equipment corresponds to the earlier instruction to sprinkle affected vessels and household contents in the corpse-purification rite.

Numbers 31:21
And Eleazar the priest said to the men of the force who were returning from the battle formation, This is the ordinance of the law that the Lord prescribed unto Moses:
  Numbers 31:22-24 — Same-chapter legal declaration and specified procedure: Eleazar's declaration introduces the named metals, fire-and-water cleansing distinctions, and seventh-day clothing wash before camp entry.

Numbers 31:22
Only the gold, and the silver, and the bronze, and the iron, and the lead, and the tin,
  Numbers 31:23 — Same-chapter materials and cleansing rule: The gold, silver, bronze, iron, lead, and tin list is followed by the distinction between objects that pass through fire and those that pass through water.

Numbers 31:23
everything that passes through fire shall also be cleansed, but it shall be purified with the water of purification. Everything that does not pass through fire shall pass through water.
  Numbers 19:17-19 — Purification water with distinct additional procedure: The purification-water requirement corresponds to the earlier ashes-and-water sprinkling rite; this war-spoil rule additionally distinguishes passage through fire and through water.

Numbers 31:24
And you shall wash your clothes on the seventh day and be cleansed; afterward you shall enter the camp.
  Numbers 19:19 — Seventh-day garment washing: Both instructions require washing clothes on the seventh day of purification; Numbers 19 also expressly specifies bodily bathing and uncleanness until evening.
  Numbers 31:19 — Same-chapter cleansing and camp return: Permission to enter after seventh-day washing completes the earlier instruction that those affected remain outside camp for seven days and receive the prescribed purification.

Numbers 31:25
And the Lord spoke unto Moses, saying:
  Numbers 31:26-30 — Same-chapter speech and spoil allocation: This speech introduces the spoil census, division into two halves, and different levies for Eleazar and the Levites from those halves.

Numbers 31:26
Take a total of the spoils of captivity, from people to livestock, you, Eleazar the priest, and the rulers of the congregation’s ancestral houses.
  Numbers 31:32-35 — Same-chapter census command and inventory: The required total of captive people and livestock is supplied by the later four-category inventory of sheep, cattle, donkeys, and women.

Numbers 31:27
And you shall divide the spoils between the warriors who go forth into battle and the whole congregation.
  Numbers 31:36-40 — Same-chapter division and warriors' half: The order to divide the spoils between warriors and congregation is implemented by the listed warriors' half and its separate levy.
  Numbers 31:42-46 — Same-chapter division and congregation's half: The second listed half belongs to the congregation, carrying out the other side of the commanded division between warriors and all Israel.
  1 Samuel 30:24-25 — War spoil shared beyond combatants: Both accounts provide a spoil share for those outside the fighting group, while Numbers divides collective halves and Samuel describes equal shares for combatants and equipment keepers.

Numbers 31:28
And you shall set apart a levy unto the Lord from the warriors who went forth into battle: one living being out of five hundred, from the men, and from the livestock, and from the cattle, and from the sheep, and from the goats.
  Numbers 31:37-40 — Same-chapter levy rule and counted results: The one-in-five-hundred levy from the warriors' half is realized in the stated sheep, cattle, donkey, and human levies.

Numbers 31:29
And you shall take it from their half; and you shall give unto Eleazar the priest the firstfruits of the Lord.
  Numbers 31:41 — Same-chapter levy recipient specified and supplied: The direction to give the Lord's portion from the warriors' half to Eleazar is fulfilled when Moses gives that levy to the priest.
  Numbers 18:8 — Priestly firstfruits grant: The war levy is called the Lord's firstfruits and given to Eleazar, corresponding to the earlier grant of consecrated firstfruits to Aaron and his descendants.

Numbers 31:30
And from the half belonging to the sons of Israel you shall take one out of fifty, from the men, and from the cattle, and from the sheep, and from the donkeys, and from all the livestock; and you shall give them unto the Levites who keep the charges in the Tent of the Lord.
  Numbers 31:47 — Same-chapter separate levy fulfilled: The congregation's one-in-fifty levy and its Levitical recipients are explicitly repeated when Moses takes that portion and delivers it to the Tent's keepers.

Numbers 31:31
And Moses and Eleazar the priest did as the Lord had prescribed unto Moses.
  Numbers 31:36-47 — Same-chapter obedience and detailed allocation: The summary that Moses and Eleazar obeyed is specified by the following division of the counted spoils and delivery of the two different levies.

Numbers 31:32
And the surplus of the plunder that the warriors had taken was six hundred seventy-five thousand sheep,
  Numbers 31:36 — Same-chapter sheep inventory and half: The inventory's sheep total of six hundred seventy-five thousand yields the warriors' half of three hundred thirty-seven thousand five hundred.

Numbers 31:33
And seventy-two thousand cattle,
  Numbers 31:38 — Same-chapter cattle total and warriors' share: The seventy-two thousand cattle are halved into the warriors' thirty-six thousand, from which the listed seventy-two levy is taken.

Numbers 31:34
And sixty-one thousand donkeys,
  Numbers 31:39 — Same-chapter donkey total and warriors' share: The sixty-one thousand donkeys are halved into thirty thousand five hundred for the warriors, followed by the stated sixty-one levy.

Numbers 31:35
and thirty-two thousand human beings in all, from the women who had not known a man’s bed.
  Numbers 31:40 — Same-chapter human total and warriors' share: The thirty-two thousand women in the kept-alive category yield the warriors' half of sixteen thousand and the listed levy of thirty-two persons.

Numbers 31:36

  Numbers 31:32 — Same-chapter warriors' sheep and inventory: The warriors' three hundred thirty-seven thousand five hundred sheep are exactly half the inventory's stated total of six hundred seventy-five thousand.
  Numbers 31:37 — Same-chapter sheep share and levy: The levy of six hundred seventy-five sheep is one five-hundredth of the warriors' three hundred thirty-seven thousand five hundred sheep.

Numbers 31:37

  Numbers 31:28-29 — Same-chapter counted sheep levy and required rate: The sheep levy belongs to the warriors' one-in-five-hundred assessment, whose designated recipient is Eleazar the priest.

Numbers 31:38

  Numbers 31:28 — Same-chapter cattle share and required rate: The stated seventy-two levy is one five-hundredth of the warriors' thirty-six thousand cattle, implementing the prescribed warriors' assessment.
  Numbers 31:33 — Same-chapter cattle share and original total: The warriors' thirty-six thousand cattle are half the earlier total of seventy-two thousand, distinct from the seventy-two animals assessed as levy.

Numbers 31:39
And the donkeys numbered thirty thousand five hundred, and the levy for the Lord was sixty-one.
  Numbers 31:28 — Same-chapter donkey share and required rate: The sixty-one donkey levy is one five-hundredth of the thirty thousand five hundred in the warriors' share, applying the stated livestock assessment.
  Numbers 31:34 — Same-chapter donkey share and original total: The thirty thousand five hundred donkeys in the warriors' half are half the earlier sixty-one-thousand total, distinct from the sixty-one levy.

Numbers 31:40

  Numbers 31:28 — Same-chapter human share and required rate: Thirty-two persons are one five-hundredth of the sixteen thousand in the warriors' human share, matching the prescribed rate for living beings.
  Numbers 31:35 — Same-chapter human share and original total: The warriors' sixteen thousand people are half the earlier thirty-two thousand women expressly identified as not having known a man's bed.

  Numbers 31:29 — Same-chapter levy delivery fulfils recipient rule: Moses gives the Lord's levy to Eleazar exactly as the earlier allocation command directs for the warriors' portion set apart.

  Numbers 31:27 — Same-chapter second half introduced: The congregation's half is introduced as separated from the warriors, implementing the command to divide spoils between those two groups.

  Numbers 31:36 — Same-chapter sheep halves compared: The congregation and warriors each receive three hundred thirty-seven thousand five hundred sheep, the two matching collective halves rather than a stated per-person division.

  Numbers 31:38 — Same-chapter cattle halves compared: The congregation's thirty-six thousand cattle match the thirty-six thousand in the warriors' half, whose separate levy is also reported.

  Numbers 31:39 — Same-chapter donkey halves compared: The congregation's thirty thousand five hundred donkeys equal the warriors' donkey half, before their distinct group levy rules are applied.

Numbers 31:46

  Numbers 31:40 — Same-chapter human halves compared: The congregation's sixteen thousand people match the warriors' human half, with the warriors' thirty-two-person levy separately stated in the target.

Numbers 31:47

  Numbers 31:30 — Same-chapter Levitical levy command fulfilled: Moses' taking one in fifty from the congregation's half and giving it to the Levites carries out the exact rate and recipient specified earlier.

  Numbers 31:5 — Same-chapter counted force and preservation report: The commanders report that no warrior in their company is missing, referring back to the force previously counted as twelve thousand equipped men.

  Numbers 31:54 — Same-chapter gift purpose and sanctuary memorial: The gold brought for atonement is received from the commanders and deposited in the Tent as a memorial for Israel before the Lord.

Numbers 32:1

Numbers 32:4

Numbers 32:6

Numbers 32:8

Numbers 32:9

Numbers 32:10

Numbers 33:1

Numbers 33:9

Numbers 33:11

Numbers 33:12

Numbers 33:13

Numbers 33:14

Numbers 33:15

Numbers 33:17

Numbers 33:18

Numbers 33:19

Numbers 33:20

Numbers 33:21

Numbers 33:22

Numbers 33:23

Numbers 33:24

Numbers 33:25

Numbers 33:26

Numbers 33:27
