Numbers 33:28
And they set out from Tarath and camped at Mithkah.
  Numbers 33:29 — Same-chapter itinerary continuation through Mithkah: The movement to Mithkah is followed by departure from Mithkah toward Zalmonah, linking this camp notice to its stated next destination.

Numbers 33:29
And they set out from Mithkah and camped at Zalmonah.
  Numbers 33:30 — Same-chapter itinerary continuation through this Zalmonah entry: This Zalmonah camp is followed by departure toward Moseroth; the comparison uses the immediate route sequence without merging the later Zalmonah occurrence after Hor.

Numbers 33:30
And they set out from Zalmonah and camped at Moseroth.
  Numbers 33:31 — Same-chapter itinerary continuation through Moseroth: Moseroth is reached from Zalmonah and then becomes the departure point toward Banaia, connecting these two stages without substituting another account's place-names.

Numbers 33:31
And they set out from Moseroth and camped at Banaia.
  Numbers 33:32 — Same-chapter itinerary continuation through Banaia: The camp at Banaia is followed by the explicit move from Banaia to Mount Gadgad, establishing the next leg from this named station.

Numbers 33:32
And they set out from Banaia and camped at Mount Gadgad.
  Deuteronomy 10:7 — Related Gadgad and Jotbathah itinerary notices: Numbers reaches Mount Gadgad before proceeding to Jotbathah in the next entry; Deuteronomy explicitly records the movement from Gadgad to Jotbathah.

Numbers 33:33
And they set out from Mount Gadgad and camped at Jotbathah.
  Deuteronomy 10:7 — Parallel named journey stage: Both describe departure from Gadgad to Jotbathah; Numbers calls the departure Mount Gadgad, while Deuteronomy describes Jotbathah as a land of flowing torrents.

Numbers 33:34
And they set out from Jotbathah and camped at Abronah.
  Numbers 33:35 — Same-chapter itinerary continuation through Abronah: After arrival at Abronah from Jotbathah, the next movement begins at Abronah and ends at Ezion-geber, giving the direct continuation of this stage.

Numbers 33:35
And they set out from Abronah and camped at Ezion-geber.
  Numbers 33:36 — Same-chapter itinerary continuation from Ezion-geber: This entry reaches Ezion-geber; the following entry departs from Ezion-geber and records the Sin and Paran/Kadesh movements, retaining its combined source unit.

Numbers 33:36
And they set out from Ezion-geber and camped in the wilderness of Sin. They set out from the wilderness of Sin and camped in the wilderness of Paran, that is, Kadesh.
  Numbers 33:37 — Same-chapter itinerary continuation from the Kadesh endpoint: This unusually combined entry ends at Paran, identified as Kadesh; the next entry takes Kadesh as the departure point toward Hor near Edom.

Numbers 33:37
And they set out from Kadesh and camped at Hor the mountain, near the land of Edom.
  Numbers 20:22-23 — Parallel Kadesh-to-Hor movement and location: Both describe the congregation's arrival at Hor from Kadesh and place the mountain near Edom, with Numbers20 introducing the subsequent divine speech there.

Numbers 33:38
And Aaron the priest went up by the Lord’s command and died there in the fortieth year of the departure of the sons of Israel from the land of Egypt, in the fifth month, on the first day of the month.
  Numbers 20:23-28 — Later itinerary and earlier narrative of Aaron's death: The itinerary dates Aaron's commanded ascent and death; the earlier account narrates the command, the garment transfer to Eleazar and Aaron's death on Hor.

Numbers 33:39
And Aaron was one hundred twenty-three years old when he died on Hor the mountain.
  Numbers 20:28-29 — Age supplement to the account of Aaron's death: This notice supplies Aaron's age of one hundred twenty-three at Hor; the earlier narrative records the mountain death and mourning without that age.

Numbers 33:40
And the Canaanite king of Arad, who lived in the land of Canaan, heard when the sons of Israel were entering.
  Numbers 21:1 — Parallel notice of the king of Arad hearing of Israel: Both name the Canaanite king of Arad hearing of Israel's approach; Numbers21 continues with his attack and the taking of captives.

Numbers 33:41
And they set out from Hor the mountain and camped at Zalmonah.
  Numbers 33:42 — Same-chapter itinerary continuation through the later Zalmonah entry: This movement from Hor reaches Zalmonah and is followed by departure from Zalmonah to Phino, without equating this occurrence with the earlier camp before Moseroth.

Numbers 33:42
And they set out from Zalmonah and camped at Phino.
  Numbers 33:43 — Same-chapter itinerary continuation through Phino: The camp at Phino is followed by the next movement from Phino to Oboth, joining the two entries at their explicitly repeated station.

Numbers 33:43
And they set out from Phino and camped at Oboth.
  Numbers 21:10 — Parallel camp at Oboth: Both notices record Israel camping at Oboth; the itinerary additionally names Phino as the preceding departure point, which the shorter narrative does not supply.

Numbers 33:44
And they set out from Oboth and camped at Gai on the other side, at the borders of Moab.
  Numbers 21:11 — Parallel Oboth departure with differing camp names: Both record leaving Oboth for a Moab-side camp; the itinerary calls it Gai and the narrative Achel-gai, without asserting an independently verified geographical name equivalence.

Numbers 33:45
And they set out from Gai and camped at Dibon-Gad.
  Numbers 33:46 — Same-chapter itinerary continuation through Dibon-Gad: This stage reaches Dibon-Gad from Gai; the following entry departs from Dibon-Gad toward Gelmon-diblathaim, establishing the station's next place in the itinerary.

Numbers 33:46
And they set out from Dibon-Gad and camped at Gelmon-diblathaim.
  Numbers 33:47 — Same-chapter itinerary continuation from Gelmon-diblathaim: Arrival at Gelmon-diblathaim is followed by departure from that same named station toward the mountains of Abarim opposite Nebo, continuing the route explicitly.

Numbers 33:47
And they set out from Gelmon-diblathaim and camped at the mountains of Abarim, opposite Nebo.
  Numbers 27:12-14 — Related mountain setting near Nebo: The itinerary locates the Abarim mountains opposite Nebo; the earlier command names Mount Nebo for Moses to see Canaan, without identifying every mountain in the itinerary with that summit.

Numbers 33:48
And they set out from the mountains of Abarim and camped on the western side of Moab, by the Jordan opposite Jericho.
  Numbers 22:1 — Parallel description of the Moab camp opposite Jericho: Both notices place Israel's camp on the western side of Moab beside the Jordan opposite Jericho, preserving the edition's actual directional wording.

Numbers 33:49
And they camped beside the Jordan from Aisimoth to Belshittim, on the western side of Moab.
  Numbers 33:48 — Same-chapter expansion of the Moab camp notice: The camp beside the Jordan is further delimited by Aisimoth and Belshittim; the preceding entry locates that Moab camp opposite Jericho.

Numbers 33:50
And the Lord spoke unto Moses on the western side of Moab, beside the Jordan opposite Jericho, saying:
  Numbers 35:1 — Repeated location of divine instructions: Both divine speech notices explicitly locate Moses on the western side of Moab beside the Jordan opposite Jericho, introducing different commands from that setting.

Numbers 33:51
Speak unto the sons of Israel, and you shall say unto them: You are crossing the Jordan into the land of Canaan,
  Numbers 34:2 — Parallel introduction to entry and land allotment: Both commands address Israel's impending entry into Canaan; Numbers34 then specifies the inheritance's boundaries rather than repeating the removal instructions that follow here.

Numbers 33:52
and you shall destroy all who inhabit the land before you, remove their lookout places, destroy all their cast idols, and remove all their pillars.
  Deuteronomy 7:1-5 — Parallel removal and cult-object destruction commands: Both passages command removal of the land's inhabitants and destruction of their cult objects; Deuteronomy specifies altars, pillars, groves and graven images.

Numbers 33:53
And you shall destroy all who inhabit the land and settle in it, for I have given you their land as an allotment.
  Deuteronomy 7:1-2 — Parallel land-gift and removal setting: Numbers links removal and settlement with the land given as an allotment; Deuteronomy similarly frames its command through the land God brings Israel to inherit.

Numbers 33:54
And you shall inherit their land by lot according to your tribes. Increase the possession of the larger groups and decrease that of the smaller groups. Wherever his name comes out shall be his. You shall inherit according to the tribes of your ancestral houses.
  Numbers 26:53-56 — Repeated proportional allotment by ancestral tribes and lot: Both passages join lots, ancestral tribal names and adjustment for larger or smaller groups; Numbers26 explicitly relates each inheritance to the registration.
  Joshua 18:4-10 — Later survey and allocation by lot: Joshua's survey and casting of lots enact the land-allocation process; Numbers prescribes allotment by tribal groups and adjusts possessions for their size.

Numbers 33:55
But if you do not destroy the inhabitants of the land from before you, those whom you leave will become splinters in your eyes and missiles in your sides, and they will act as enemies in the land where you shall settle.
  Joshua 23:12-13 — Later warning with closely related bodily imagery: Numbers warns that remaining inhabitants become splinters in eyes, missiles in sides and enemies; Joshua warns of traps, nails in heels and darts in eyes.
  Judges 2:2-3 — Later warning about the remaining inhabitants: The earlier warning predicts hostility from those left in the land; Judges connects disobedience over the inhabitants and their gods with constraints and stumbling blocks.

Numbers 33:56
And then I will do to you what I had determined to do to them.
  Leviticus 18:24-28 — Warning that Israel may suffer the preceding inhabitants' judgment: Numbers threatens Israel with the treatment intended for the inhabitants; Leviticus warns that Israel's own defilement can bring the land's rejection experienced by the former nations.

Numbers 34:1
And the Lord spoke unto Moses, saying:
  Numbers 34:2 — Same-chapter speech introduction and boundary commission: This divine speech notice introduces the command defining Canaan as Israel's inheritance and specifying its boundaries in the following instructions.

Numbers 34:2
Command the sons of Israel, and you shall say unto them: You are entering the land of Canaan; this shall be your inheritance, the land of Canaan with its boundaries.
  Joshua 14:1-5 — Later allotment of the named Canaan inheritance: Numbers introduces Canaan's inheritance and boundaries; Joshua describes Eleazar, Joshua and the tribal heads allotting that inheritance according to the command.

Numbers 34:3
And your southwestern side shall run from the wilderness of Sin to the territory adjoining Edom. Your southwestern boundary shall begin from the part of the Salt Sea toward the east.
  Joshua 15:1-2 — Parallel southwestern boundary landmarks: Both descriptions place the southwestern boundary near Edom or Idumea and begin from the Salt Sea's edge; each retains its actual Sin/Zin wording.

Numbers 34:4
And the boundary shall turn around you from the southwest toward the Ascent of Akrabin and pass through Senna. Its endpoint shall be southwest of Kadesh-barnea; it shall extend to the settlement of Arad and pass through Azmon.
  Joshua 15:3-4 — Boundary comparison with shared landmarks and distinct additional names: Both trace Akrabin, Senna, Kadesh-barnea and Azmon; Numbers retains its Arad wording while Joshua includes Hezron and Addara, without silently merging the additional names.

Numbers 34:5
And the boundary shall turn from Azmon to the Wadi of Egypt, and its endpoint shall be the sea.
  Joshua 15:4 — Parallel Azmon-to-Egypt-ravine boundary ending at the sea: Both boundary descriptions run from Azmon to Egypt's watercourse and end at the sea, with Numbers saying Wadi and Joshua ravine.

Numbers 34:6
And for your sea boundary, the Great Sea shall mark the limit. This shall be your sea boundary.
  Ezekiel 47:20 — Related sea-facing boundary descriptions: Both use the Great Sea as the sea-facing boundary; Ezekiel additionally relates that boundary to the entrance of Hamath in its own land description.

Numbers 34:7
And this shall be your boundary toward the north: from the Great Sea you shall measure out for yourselves beside the mountain, the mountain;
  Ezekiel 47:15 — Related northern boundary beginning at the Great Sea: Both northern descriptions begin from the Great Sea; Numbers retains the repeated unnamed mountain wording, while Ezekiel proceeds toward Hamath without naming that mountain.

Numbers 34:8
and from the mountain, the mountain, you shall measure out for them toward the entrance into Hamath; and its endpoint shall be the boundaries of Sarada.
  Ezekiel 47:15 — Related Hamath entrance in northern boundary descriptions: Both descriptions explicitly include the entrance into Hamath; Numbers' Sarada and Ezekiel's Seddada remain their distinct written forms without an asserted name equivalence.

Numbers 34:9
And the boundary shall extend to Dephrona, and its endpoint shall be Hazar-enan. This shall be your northern boundary.
  Numbers 34:10 — Same-chapter meeting of northern and eastern boundaries: Hazar-enan ends the northern boundary and then becomes the starting point of the eastern boundary toward Shepham, linking the two stated border sections.

Numbers 34:10
And you shall measure out for yourselves the eastern boundary from Hazar-enan unto Shepham.
  Numbers 34:9 — Same-chapter eastern boundary beginning at the northern endpoint: The eastward description starts at Hazar-enan, which the preceding verse identifies as the north boundary's endpoint, before proceeding to Shepham.

Numbers 34:11
And the boundary shall descend from Shepham to Arbela, from the east toward the springs. The boundary shall descend to Bela, along the eastern ridge of the Sea of Chennara.
  Numbers 34:12 — Same-chapter continuation of the eastern boundary: The descent past Arbela, Bela and the Sea of Chennara continues in the next verse to the Jordan and Salt Sea; no familiar alternate place-names replace these forms.

Numbers 34:12
And the boundary shall descend to the Jordan, and its endpoint shall be the Salt Sea. This shall be your land and its surrounding boundaries.
  Ezekiel 47:18 — Boundary comparison involving the Jordan with distinct sea descriptions: Both eastern border descriptions include the Jordan; Numbers ends at the Salt Sea, while Ezekiel describes the sea east of Palm Grove without an asserted name equation.

Numbers 34:13
And Moses commanded the sons of Israel, saying: This is the land that you shall inherit by lot, as the Lord prescribed unto Moses to give to the nine tribes and the half-tribe of Manasseh.
  Joshua 14:1-3 — Later allotment by lot to nine and a half tribes: Both passages identify an allotment by lots involving nine tribes and a half-tribe; Joshua describes the distributing leaders and preserves its own beyond-Jordan wording.

Numbers 34:14
For the tribe of the sons of Reuben and the tribe of the sons of Gad according to their ancestral houses, and the half-tribe of Manasseh, have received their allotments.
  Numbers 32:33 — Earlier grant to the eastern tribes: The boundary summary says Reuben, Gad and half-Manasseh already received allotments; the earlier grant names these recipients and the kingdoms of Sihon and Og.

Numbers 34:15
The two tribes and the half-tribe have received their allotments across the Jordan opposite Jericho, from the south toward the east.
  Joshua 18:7 — Later recognition of the eastern allotments already given by Moses: Both distinguish Gad, Reuben and half-Manasseh as recipients of land across the Jordan; Joshua explicitly describes their inheritance as eastward and given by Moses.

Numbers 34:16
And the Lord spoke unto Moses, saying:
  Numbers 34:17-18 — Same-chapter introduction and appointment of land distributors: The divine speech notice introduces Eleazar and Joshua and the requirement to take a ruler from each tribe for distributing the land.

Numbers 34:17
These are the names of the men who shall obtain the land as an inheritance for you: Eleazar the priest and Joshua son of Nun.
  Joshua 14:1 — Named appointment and later distribution of the inheritance: Eleazar the priest and Joshua son of Nun are appointed here; Joshua later names both among those assigning the Canaan inheritance with the tribal heads.

Numbers 34:18
And you shall take one ruler from each tribe to allot the land unto you.
  Joshua 19:51 — Appointment and later activity of tribal distribution leaders: Numbers calls for a ruler from each tribe; Joshua's summary names the ancestral tribal leaders alongside Eleazar and Joshua in allotment by lots.

Numbers 34:19
And these are the names of the men: from the tribe of Judah, Caleb son of Jephunneh;
  Numbers 13:6 — Earlier identification of Caleb with the same tribe and patronymic: Both lists identify Caleb son of Jephunneh from Judah; the earlier scout list and this distribution roster assign him different stated responsibilities.

Numbers 34:20
from the tribe of Simeon, Salamiel son of Ammihud;
  Numbers 34:18 — Same-chapter tribal representative and his stated commission: Salamiel son of Ammihud is named for Simeon in the distribution roster, supplying that tribe's representative under the one-ruler-per-tribe instruction.

Numbers 34:21
from the tribe of Benjamin, Eldad son of Chislon;
  Numbers 34:18 — Same-chapter tribal representative and his stated commission: Eldad son of Chislon represents Benjamin in the named land-distribution list, fulfilling the instruction to take a ruler from each tribe without inferring another Eldad's identity.

Numbers 34:22
from the tribe of Dan, the ruler Bakchir son of Egli;
  Numbers 34:18 — Same-chapter named ruler and tribal distribution role: Bakchir son of Egli is explicitly called Dan's ruler; the introductory rule assigns a ruler from each tribe to participate in allotting the land.

Numbers 34:23
from the descendants of Joseph, from the tribe of the sons of Manasseh, the ruler Hanniel son of Ouphi;
  Numbers 34:17-18 — Same-chapter Manasseh representative within the distribution commission: Hanniel son of Ouphi is named for Manasseh among Joseph's descendants, alongside the commission assigning tribal rulers to assist Eleazar and Joshua in inheritance distribution.

Numbers 34:24
from the tribe of the sons of Ephraim, the ruler Kemuel son of Sabatha;
  Numbers 34:17-18 — Same-chapter Ephraim representative within the distribution commission: Kemuel son of Sabatha is named for Ephraim; the preceding commission explains the land-allotment task of these tribal representatives under Eleazar and Joshua.

Numbers 34:25
from the tribe of Zebulun, the ruler Elizaphan son of Parnach;
  Numbers 34:18 — Same-chapter Zebulun representative and tribal commission: Elizaphan son of Parnach is named for Zebulun in the roster, providing its particular representative for the instruction to appoint tribal land distributors.

Numbers 34:26
from the tribe of the sons of Issachar, the ruler Paltiel son of Oza;
  Numbers 34:18 — Same-chapter Issachar representative and tribal commission: Paltiel son of Oza is named for Issachar, giving the particular ruler associated with that tribe in the preceding land-distribution instruction.

Numbers 34:27
from the tribe of the sons of Asher, the ruler Achior son of Shelomi;
  Numbers 34:18 — Same-chapter Asher representative and tribal commission: Achior son of Shelomi is named for Asher in the distribution roster; the earlier command explains why a representative is supplied for this tribe.

Numbers 34:28
from the tribe of Naphtali, the ruler Pedahel son of Benammihud.
  Numbers 34:18 — Same-chapter Naphtali representative and tribal commission: Pedahel son of Benammihud is the named Naphtali representative, completing the roster that supplies tribal rulers for the specified land-allotment task.

Numbers 34:29
These are the men whom the Lord commanded to distribute portions to the sons of Israel in the land of Canaan.
  Joshua 19:51 — Commission summary and later allotment summary: Numbers closes the commissioned distributors' list with their task of assigning Canaan's portions; Joshua summarizes Eleazar, Joshua and the tribal heads doing that work by lots.

Numbers 35:1
And the Lord spoke unto Moses on the western side of Moab, beside the Jordan opposite Jericho, saying:
  Numbers 33:50 — Repeated Moab location of divine instructions: Both speech notices place Moses on the western side of Moab by the Jordan opposite Jericho, introducing distinct instructions in the same stated setting.

Numbers 35:2
Instruct the sons of Israel to give the Levites towns to live in from their allotted possessions. They shall also give the Levites the outlying lands around the towns.
  Joshua 21:1-3 — Command and later request for Levite towns: The Levites later appeal to the Mosaic command for cities and surrounding livestock lands; Joshua records Israel giving those places from the inheritance.

Numbers 35:3
And the towns shall be theirs to live in, and their designated lands shall be for their livestock and all their four-footed animals.
  Joshua 21:2 — Explicit later recollection of the purpose of Levite towns and lands: Numbers assigns towns for residence and adjoining lands for livestock; the Levite request in Joshua explicitly recalls those two purposes of the command.
  Leviticus 25:32-34 — Complementary rules for Levite town property: Numbers supplies towns and their livestock lands; Leviticus preserves Levite redemption rights in their town houses and forbids sale of the designated fields.

Numbers 35:4
And the lands adjoining the towns that you shall give the Levites shall extend two thousand cubits outward from the town wall all around.
  Numbers 35:5 — Same-chapter surrounding-land measurement and directional specification: Both verses specify two thousand cubits in this edition; the following verse gives the four measured sides with the town in the midst, without importing a different first distance.

Numbers 35:5
And you shall measure outside the town two thousand cubits on the eastern side, and two thousand cubits on the southwestern side, and two thousand cubits on the seaward side, and two thousand cubits on the northern side; and the town shall be in the midst thereof for you, together with the adjoining lands of the towns.
  Numbers 35:4 — Same-chapter directional measurement and preceding wall-based extent: The eastern, southwestern, seaward and northern two-thousand-cubit measurements expand the preceding wall-based two-thousand-cubit extent of the Levite towns' adjoining lands.

Numbers 35:6
And you shall give the Levites the six cities of refuge that you shall provide for the person who has killed someone to flee to, and in addition to these, forty-two towns.
  Numbers 35:7 — Same-chapter component counts and stated total: The six refuge towns and forty-two additional towns make up the following total of forty-eight Levite towns with their adjoining lands.
  Joshua 20:7-9 — Command for six refuge towns and later named designation: Numbers specifies six refuges among the Levite towns; Joshua names three on each side of the Jordan and states their purpose for involuntary killing.

Numbers 35:7
All the towns you shall give the Levites shall be forty-eight towns, together with their outlying lands.
  Joshua 21:41-42 — Commanded total and later allotment total: Both passages explicitly give forty-eight Levite towns with surrounding lands, with Joshua describing the allotment's resulting total and each town's adjoining land.

Numbers 35:8
And for the towns that you shall give from the possession of the sons of Israel, take many from those with much and fewer from those with less. Each shall give towns to the Levites according to the inheritance he receives.
  Numbers 26:54 — Related proportional rules for inheritance and Levite contributions: The Levite towns are contributed in proportion to each tribe's possession; the earlier inheritance rule likewise enlarges or reduces allotments for more or fewer people.

Numbers 35:9
And the Lord spoke unto Moses, saying:
  Numbers 35:10-12 — Same-chapter introduction and specified refuge instructions: The divine speech notice introduces the crossing into Canaan, appointment of refuges for unintentional killers and protection until congregational judgment.

Numbers 35:10
Speak unto the sons of Israel, and you shall say unto them: You are crossing the Jordan into the land of Canaan,
  Deuteronomy 19:1-3 — Related entry-and-settlement setting for refuge provision: Both place the appointment of refuge towns within possession of the promised land; Deuteronomy specifies three towns and marked routes within the territory.

Numbers 35:11
and you shall set apart towns for yourselves. They shall be refuges for the killer to flee there, anyone who has struck a person unintentionally.
  Exodus 21:13 — Refuge provision for killing without willing intent: Numbers assigns towns for the person who kills unintentionally; Exodus promises a place of flight for the slayer who did not act willingly.
  Deuteronomy 19:4-6 — Parallel conditions of unintentional killing and protective flight: Numbers appoints refuge for unintentional killing; Deuteronomy additionally specifies no previous hatred and illustrates this through the axe accident and the avenger's pursuit.

Numbers 35:12
And the towns shall be refuges for you from the kinsman who avenges blood, so that the killer shall in no wise die before standing before the congregation for judgment.
  Joshua 20:2-3 — Explicit later restatement of refuge until congregational judgment: Joshua recalls the refuge instruction through Moses and repeats that the involuntary killer must not die by the avenger before standing for congregational judgment.

Numbers 35:13
And the towns you shall provide, the six towns, shall be refuges for you.
  Joshua 20:7-9 — Six-town requirement and later designation of the refuges: The rule fixes six refuge towns; Joshua's designation lists three westward locations and three beyond the Jordan, then states their shared refuge purpose.

Numbers 35:14
You shall provide three towns across the Jordan, and you shall provide three towns in the land of Canaan.
  Joshua 20:7-8 — Three-and-three distribution and its later named implementation: Numbers divides the refuges into three across the Jordan and three in Canaan; Joshua names the two groups with their respective tribal settings.

Numbers 35:15
There shall be a refuge for the sons of Israel; for the sojourner and the resident stranger among you, these towns shall also be refuges, so that anyone who strikes a person unintentionally may flee there.
  Joshua 20:9 — Parallel refuge access for Israelites and sojourners: Both extend protection for involuntary killing beyond Israelites to the sojourner among them, with Joshua restating protection from the blood-avenging next of kin until judgment.

Numbers 35:16
But if he strikes someone with an iron object and that person dies, he is a murderer. Let the murderer surely be put to death.
  Exodus 21:12 — Material-specific fatal blow beside the general fatal-striking rule: Numbers classifies a fatal iron-object blow as murder requiring death; Exodus states the general rule for striking another person so that he dies.

Numbers 35:17
If he strikes someone with a stone from his hand capable of causing death and that person dies, he is a murderer. Let the murderer surely be put to death.
  Numbers 35:18 — Same-chapter parallel lethal-object cases: The stone case and the following wooden-object case both specify an object capable of causing death, actual death, the murder classification and the death requirement.

Numbers 35:18
If he strikes someone with a wooden object from his hand capable of causing death and that person dies, he is a murderer. Let the murderer surely be put to death.
  Exodus 21:12 — Wooden-object fatal blow beside the general fatal-striking rule: Numbers specifies a deadly wooden object and the victim's death; Exodus gives the broader rule requiring death when one person's blow kills another.

Numbers 35:19
The kinsman who avenges blood shall himself kill the murderer. When he encounters him, he shall kill him.
  Deuteronomy 19:11-12 — Parallel role of the blood avenger in the murder case: Numbers assigns the murderer to the blood-avenging kinsman; Deuteronomy requires city elders to hand the hateful ambush-killer to that avenger rather than shelter him.

Numbers 35:20
If he pushes someone out of enmity and throws an object at him from ambush and that person dies,
  Numbers 35:21 — Same-chapter hostile-action conditions and murder consequence: The enmity and ambush conditions leading to death are continued by the wrathful blow case and the stated murder penalty and avenger's role.

Numbers 35:21
or strikes him with his hand out of wrath and he dies, let the one who struck him surely be put to death; he is a murderer. Let the murderer surely be put to death. The kinsman who avenges blood shall kill the murderer when he encounters him.
  Deuteronomy 19:11-13 — Parallel exclusion of hostile murder from protective refuge: The wrathful fatal attacker is classed as a murderer; Deuteronomy likewise denies protection to the hateful ambush-killer and hands him to the avenger.

Numbers 35:22
But if he suddenly pushes someone without enmity, or throws any object at him without ambush,
  Deuteronomy 19:4-6 — Parallel absence-of-enmity condition for the involuntary killer: Numbers distinguishes a sudden push without enmity or a throw without ambush; Deuteronomy likewise conditions protective refuge on unintentional killing without previous hatred.

Numbers 35:23
or, without his knowing, a stone capable of causing death falls upon him and he dies, though he was not his enemy and was not seeking to harm him,
  Deuteronomy 19:4-6 — Parallel accidental death without prior hostility: The unnoticed falling stone and Deuteronomy's slipping axe iron illustrate unintended fatal harm without hostility, rather than the murderer cases described alongside them.

Numbers 35:24
And then the congregation shall judge between the one who struck him and the kinsman who avenges blood according to these judgments.
  Joshua 20:3 — Parallel requirement of congregational judgment: Numbers assigns the dispute between killer and avenger to congregational judgment; Joshua's refuge notice explicitly preserves the requirement to stand before the congregation.

Numbers 35:25
And the congregation shall rescue the killer from the kinsman who avenges blood and restore him to the city of refuge to which he fled. He shall live there until the high priest dies, whom they anointed with the holy oil.
  Numbers 35:28 — Same-chapter refuge residence and the high-priest-death limit: The rescued killer remains in refuge until the anointed high priest dies; the later rule repeats that limit and explicitly permits return to his possession afterward.

Numbers 35:26
But if the killer goes outside the boundaries of the city to which he fled,
  Numbers 35:27-28 — Same-chapter departure condition and its legal consequences: Leaving the refuge boundary introduces the next verse's avenger exception; the accompanying explanation states the residence requirement until the high priest's death.

Numbers 35:27
and the kinsman who avenges blood finds him outside the boundaries of his city of refuge and kills the killer, the avenger is not liable.
  Numbers 35:26 — Same-chapter avenger exception within the boundary rule: The avenger's lack of liability depends on finding the killer outside the refuge; the preceding condition explicitly concerns the killer leaving the city's boundary.

Numbers 35:28
For he must live in the city of refuge until the high priest dies. After the death of the high priest, the killer may return to the land of his possession.
  Numbers 35:25 — Same-chapter restatement of refuge residence until priestly death: The return-to-possession rule follows the earlier instruction to restore the killer to refuge until the high priest dies, preserving the same release condition.

Numbers 35:29
And these shall be an ordinance of judgment for you throughout your generations in all your settlements.
  Numbers 35:24 — Same-chapter enduring judgment ordinance and its judicial procedure: The ordinance's enduring scope includes the congregation judging between the killer and avenger according to these judgments, linking the legal summary to its specified procedure.

Numbers 35:30
In every case of a person being struck down, you shall put the killer to death through witnesses. A single witness shall not testify against a person so as to cause his death.
  Deuteronomy 17:6-7 — Parallel rejection of single-witness execution: Both forbid execution on a lone witness's testimony; Deuteronomy specifies two or three witnesses and their role at the beginning of the execution.
  Deuteronomy 19:15 — Related evidentiary rule against a single witness: The capital case requires witnesses rather than a single accuser; Deuteronomy states the wider two-or-three-witness requirement for establishing a matter.

Numbers 35:31
And you shall not accept a ransom for a life from the killer who is guilty and liable to be killed; he shall surely be put to death.
  Numbers 35:16-18 — Same-chapter ransom prohibition and the stated murder penalty: The refusal of ransom preserves the death requirement attached to the fatal iron, stone and wooden-object murder cases, rather than substituting payment for the penalty.

Numbers 35:32
You shall not accept a ransom concerning flight to a city of refuge, allowing him to live in the land again before the high priest dies.
  Numbers 35:28 — Same-chapter ransom prohibition and the stated refuge-release condition: The prohibition bars payment allowing early return to the land; the underlying rule permits return to possession only after the high priest's death.

Numbers 35:33
And you shall in no wise stain with murder the land wherein you dwell; for this blood stains the land with murder, and atonement shall not be made for the land for the blood shed upon it, except by the blood of him who shed it.
  Leviticus 18:24-28 — Related warning about defilement of the inhabited land: Numbers identifies shed blood as land-defiling and specifies its atonement condition; Leviticus likewise warns that defilement brings judgment upon the land's inhabitants, without equating the offenses.

Numbers 35:34
And you shall not defile the land in which you dwell, in which I will dwell among you, for I am the Lord, dwelling among the sons of Israel.
  Numbers 5:3 — Particular no-defilement command grounded in divine dwelling: Both prohibit defiling the place where the Lord dwells among Israel; Numbers5 applies this to camps, while this instruction concerns the inhabited land in its bloodshed context.

Numbers 36:1
And the rulers of the tribe of the descendants of Gilead son of Machir, son of Manasseh, from the tribe of Joseph’s descendants, approached and spoke before Moses, Eleazar the priest, and the rulers of the ancestral houses of the sons of Israel.
  Numbers 27:1-2 — Related appearance of the same ancestral inheritance constituency: Both cases identify Zelophehad's Gilead-Machir-Manasseh ancestry and come before Moses, Eleazar and the rulers; here the clan leaders raise the later tribal inheritance concern.

Numbers 36:2
And they said: The Lord commanded our lord to distribute the inherited land by lot to the sons of Israel, and the Lord instructed the lord to give the inheritance of our brother Zelophehad to his daughters.
  Numbers 27:7-8 — Explicit recollection of the daughters' inheritance ruling: The clan leaders recall God's instruction to grant Zelophehad's daughters their father's inheritance; the earlier ruling explicitly gives them that possession and establishes daughter succession.
  Numbers 26:55-56 — Explicit recollection of allocation by tribal lot: The complaint recalls the commanded distribution by lot; the earlier allocation rule specifically assigns inheritance by ancestral tribal names and the lot.

Numbers 36:3
And if they become wives in one of the Israelite tribes, their allotment will be taken from our fathers’ possession and added to the inheritance of the tribe into which they marry. It will be taken from our inherited allotment.
  Numbers 36:7-9 — Same-chapter stated problem and rule preventing tribal transfer: The leaders describe marriage transferring a daughter's allotment between tribes; the response forbids such transfers and requires an heiress to marry within her father's clan.

Numbers 36:4
If the release comes for the sons of Israel, their inheritance will be added to the inheritance of the tribe into which they marry, and their inheritance will be taken from the inheritance of our ancestral tribe.
  Leviticus 25:24-28 — Inheritance concern in relation to the release legislation: The leaders raise the effect of the release on tribal inheritance; Leviticus describes land redemption and return at the release without supplying the marriage remedy given here.

Numbers 36:5
And Moses commanded the sons of Israel by the Lord’s instruction, saying: Thus speaks the tribe of Joseph’s descendants.
  Numbers 36:6-9 — Same-chapter divine-authorized response and its content: Moses' command introduces the specific marriage choice within the father's clan and the prohibition of inheritance passing between tribes.

Numbers 36:6
This is the word that the Lord prescribed concerning Zelophehad’s daughters: Let them marry whom they please, but they shall marry from their father’s clan.
  Numbers 36:11-12 — Same-chapter marriage instruction and reported compliance: The rule permits choice within the paternal clan; the later report names the daughters marrying cousins within Manasseh and the inheritance remaining with that tribe.

Numbers 36:7
And no inheritance among the sons of Israel shall pass from tribe to tribe, for each Israelite shall hold fast to the inheritance of his ancestral tribe.
  Numbers 36:3 — Same-chapter protective rule and the original transfer concern: Holding fast to ancestral tribal inheritance directly addresses the leaders' concern that marriage into another tribe would remove the daughters' allotment from their fathers' possession.

Numbers 36:8
And every daughter who succeeds as next of kin to an inheritance among the Israelite tribes shall marry someone from her father’s clan, so that each Israelite may succeed as next of kin to his paternal inheritance.
  Numbers 27:8-11 — Complementary heiress-marriage and inheritance-succession rules: The earlier rule assigns a sonless man's property to his daughter and then other kin; this rule constrains an inheriting daughter's marriage to preserve the paternal tribal possession.

Numbers 36:9
And no allotment shall pass from one tribe to another; rather, the sons of Israel shall each hold fast to their own inheritance.
  Numbers 36:12 — Same-chapter nontransfer rule and the narrated outcome: The prohibition of allotments passing between tribes is followed by the statement that the daughters' marriages kept their inheritance within their father's tribe.

Numbers 36:10
Zelophehad’s daughters did as the Lord had prescribed unto Moses.
  Numbers 36:6 — Same-chapter compliance notice and the specific marriage command: The daughters' obedience refers back to the instruction allowing them to marry whom they please within their father's clan, rather than to an unrestricted marriage choice.

Numbers 36:11
And Tirzah, Hoglah, Milcah, Noah, and Mahlah, the daughters of Zelophehad, married their cousins.
  Numbers 27:1 — Same named daughters in the initial and final inheritance accounts: Both passages name Zelophehad's five daughters, retaining their different list order; the final notice reports the cousin marriages following the earlier inheritance petition.

Numbers 36:12
They married within the clan of Manasseh, of Joseph’s descendants, and their inheritance remained with the tribe of their father’s clan.
  Numbers 36:7-9 — Same-chapter outcome fulfilling the tribal retention rules: Marrying within Manasseh leaves the inheritance in the paternal clan's tribe, giving the concrete outcome sought by the repeated prohibition on intertribal transfer.

Numbers 36:13
These are the commandments, ordinances, and judgments that the Lord commanded through Moses on the western side of Moab, by the Jordan opposite Jericho.
  Numbers 35:1 — Same-location closing summary and preceding instruction setting: The closing commandments summary repeats the western Moab, Jordan and opposite-Jericho setting of the earlier speech introducing the Levite-town instructions.
  Deuteronomy 1:3-5 — Following book's Moab setting of Mosaic instruction: The Numbers conclusion locates divine commands through Moses in Moab by the Jordan; Deuteronomy introduces Moses explaining the commanded law beyond the Jordan in Moab.
